Chapter 1 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 3, "bbox_points": [291.3280944824219, 113.62800598144531, 359.6719055175781, 126.9120101928711]}]
Introduction 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [108.0, 164.8199920654297, 192.35159301757812, 178.27198791503906]}]
1.1 Background 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [108.0, 197.8679962158203, 542.98876953125, 211.15199279785156]}]
Anaerobic digestion (AD), a process originally used for treating municipal and industrial 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [108.0, 218.5080108642578, 542.998779296875, 231.79200744628906]}]
wastewaters, was successfully adopted for bioenergy production using diverse feedstocks 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [108.0, 239.1480255126953, 542.9949340820312, 252.43202209472656]}]
such as sewage sludge, municipal solid waste, food wastes, agricultural residues, animal 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 9, "bbox_points": [108.0, 260.0279846191406, 542.9990234375, 273.3119812011719]}]
manures and energy crops. The major advantages of AD process include low energy 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [108.0, 280.6679992675781, 543.0, 293.9519958496094]}]
consumption, sequestration of greenhouse gases, and above all, its ability to remediate 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 11, "bbox_points": [108.0, 301.3080139160156, 543.0, 314.5920104980469]}]
diverse organic wastes with concomitant production of renewable energy, biogas (Khanal 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [108.0, 321.9480285644531, 542.9957275390625, 335.2320251464844]}]
and Li, 2016). The applications of biogas from AD plants for heat and electricity 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 13, "bbox_points": [108.0, 342.8280029296875, 542.9981079101562, 356.11199951171875]}]
generation or upgrading into natural gas for injection into natural gas grid, have been 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 14, "bbox_points": [108.0, 363.468017578125, 543.0, 376.75201416015625]}]
widely practiced, especially in European countries, where there are over 17,376 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 15, "bbox_points": [108.0, 384.1080017089844, 543.0, 397.3919982910156]}]
commercial biogas plants currently in generating 8,293 MW of electricity equivalent 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 16, "bbox_points": [108.0, 404.7480163574219, 543.0, 418.0320129394531]}]
(EBA, 2018). In the United States, there are currently 266 farm-based and 1,270 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 17, "bbox_points": [108.0, 425.6280212402344, 542.9984130859375, 438.9120178222656]}]
municipal sludge treatment-based anaerobic digesters in operation (EPA, 2018; WEF, 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 18, "bbox_points": [108.0, 446.26800537109375, 543.0, 459.552001953125]}]
2018). However, 63 farm-based AD plants among total of 266 plants were shut down, 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 19, "bbox_points": [108.0, 466.90802001953125, 545.9954833984375, 480.1920166015625]}]
and many other AD plants are on the verge of closing down mostly due to economic issue  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 20, "bbox_points": [108.0, 487.5480041503906, 466.6054992675781, 500.8320007324219]}]
caused by the process instability and low biogas production (EPA, 2018).  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 21, "bbox_points": [108.0, 520.427978515625, 542.999267578125, 533.7119750976562]}]
Slow digestibility, especially when fed with recalcitrant lignocellulosic biomass, is one of 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 22, "bbox_points": [108.0, 541.0679931640625, 542.9962158203125, 554.3519897460938]}]
the main reasons for low biogas production (Sawatdeenarunat et al., 2015). To overcome 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 23, "bbox_points": [108.0, 561.7080078125, 543.0003051757812, 574.9920043945312]}]
this limitation, digesters are commonly operated at high organic loading rates (OLRs) 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 24, "bbox_points": [108.0, 582.3479614257812, 542.9987182617188, 595.6319580078125]}]
using substrates with a high total solids (TS) content. However, at high OLRs with high 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 25, "bbox_points": [108.0, 602.9879760742188, 542.99267578125, 616.27197265625]}]
TS content, digesters are susceptible to failure due to accumulation of volatile fatty acids 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 26, "bbox_points": [108.0, 623.8679809570312, 543.0, 637.1519775390625]}]
(VFA) (mainly acetic, propionic and butyric acids) caused by kinetic and energetic 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 27, "bbox_points": [108.0, 644.5079956054688, 543.0, 657.7919921875]}]
imbalance between fermentative acidogens, syntrophic acetogens and methanogens 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 28, "bbox_points": [108.0, 665.14794921875, 375.9375, 678.4319458007812]}]
(Leng et al., 2017; Pind et al., 2003; Treu et al., 2016).  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 29, "bbox_points": [108.0, 691.7879638671875, 542.9990844726562, 705.0719604492188]}]
Automatic AD process control system enables quick process stabilization by closely 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 12, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 30, "bbox_points": [318.0, 729.4679565429688, 330.0, 742.751953125]}]
 12


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 1, "bbox_points": [108.0, 72.34797668457031, 542.9979248046875, 85.6319808959961]}]
monitoring and controlling the process. First, the process parameters/indicators that are 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 2, "bbox_points": [108.0, 92.98799133300781, 543.0, 106.2719955444336]}]
sensitive to process disturbances, such as pH, biogas production rate, VFA concentration, 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 3, "bbox_points": [108.0, 113.62800598144531, 542.9864501953125, 126.9120101928711]}]
or total VFA to total alkalinity (VFA/ALK) ratio are closely monitored. The monitoring 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 4, "bbox_points": [108.0, 134.5080108642578, 543.0, 147.79200744628906]}]
data is then sent to control system to regulate these parameters within the desired ranges 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [108.0, 155.1480255126953, 542.993896484375, 168.43202209472656]}]
either by adding chemicals to control the pH or regulating feeding rate using diverse 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [108.0, 175.78797912597656, 542.9984130859375, 189.0719757080078]}]
process control systems (Nguyen et al., 2015). Stopping or reducing the substrate feeding 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [108.0, 196.42799377441406, 542.9913330078125, 209.7119903564453]}]
rate allows microbes in the AD process to consume the accumulated metabolites and 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [108.0, 217.30799865722656, 542.995849609375, 230.5919952392578]}]
undigested substrates in the reactor and slowly overcome organic overloading condition. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 9, "bbox_points": [108.0, 237.94801330566406, 542.999267578125, 251.2320098876953]}]
However, cessation of substrate feeding leads to piling-up of feedstock that needs to be 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [108.0, 258.5880432128906, 542.9946899414062, 271.8720397949219]}]
digested and also reduces profits associated with biogas applications. Therefore, there is a 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 11, "bbox_points": [108.0, 279.22802734375, 542.9926147460938, 292.51202392578125]}]
critical need to find an alternative controlled strategy that allows reactor to stably operate 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [108.0, 300.1080017089844, 542.9989013671875, 313.3919982910156]}]
at a higher OLR without the need of adding chemical or compromising the substrate 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 13, "bbox_points": [108.0, 320.5080261230469, 173.9647979736328, 333.7920227050781]}]
feeding rate.  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 14, "bbox_points": [108.0, 351.468017578125, 542.99951171875, 364.75201416015625]}]
To this end, we developed a unique approach of improving the digester stability through 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 15, "bbox_points": [108.0, 372.1080017089844, 542.9971923828125, 385.3919982910156]}]
intermittent injection of small amount of oxygen (micro-aeration), by controlling the 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 16, "bbox_points": [108.0, 392.7480163574219, 543.0009155273438, 406.0320129394531]}]
oxidation-reduction potential (ORP) of the AD process. Recent studies have also shown 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 17, "bbox_points": [108.0, 413.6280212402344, 542.9942016601562, 426.9120178222656]}]
that micro-aeration in anaerobic processes enhances hydrogen sulfide removal from 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 18, "bbox_points": [108.0, 434.26800537109375, 543.0, 447.552001953125]}]
biogas (Díaz et al., 2011b; Khanal and Huang, 2006; Krayzelova et al., 2015), facilitates 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 19, "bbox_points": [108.0, 454.90802001953125, 543.0, 468.1920166015625]}]
hydrolysis (Lim and Wang, 2013; Xu et al., 2014a), enhances VFA production 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 20, "bbox_points": [108.0, 475.5480041503906, 542.99658203125, 488.8320007324219]}]
(Jagadabhi et al., 2010a; Sawatdeenarunat et al., 2017), and improves methane production 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 21, "bbox_points": [108.0, 496.4280090332031, 542.9968872070312, 509.7120056152344]}]
(Nguyen et al., 2007, Lim and Wang, 2013). The beneficial effects of micro-aeration in 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 22, "bbox_points": [108.0, 517.0679931640625, 542.9794921875, 530.3519897460938]}]
AD processes is due to the augmentation in diversity and activity of facultative bacteria 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 23, "bbox_points": [108.0, 537.7080078125, 542.9996337890625, 550.9920043945312]}]
that promote hydrolysis, fermentation, and sulfide oxidation (Krayzelova et al., 2015; 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 24, "bbox_points": [108.0, 558.3479614257812, 543.0, 571.6319580078125]}]
Lim et al., 2014; Zhu et al., 2009). Based on this reasoning, we hypothesize that VFA 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 25, "bbox_points": [108.0, 578.9879760742188, 542.9963989257812, 592.27197265625]}]
accumulated in the digesters during overloading conditions are rapidly consumed by 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 26, "bbox_points": [108.0, 599.8679809570312, 542.9996948242188, 613.1519775390625]}]
facultative heterotrophs under micro-aerobic conditions thus contributing to AD process 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 27, "bbox_points": [108.0, 620.2680053710938, 155.66600036621094, 633.552001953125]}]
stability.  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 28, "bbox_points": [108.0, 653.14794921875, 542.9994506835938, 666.4319458007812]}]
Few studies focused on an effective micro-aeration dosing system that can be applied to a 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 29, "bbox_points": [108.0, 673.7879638671875, 542.998291015625, 687.0719604492188]}]
mix-culture AD process or effect of micro-aeration on reducing VFA accumulation in the 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 30, "bbox_points": [108.0, 694.66796875, 542.9979858398438, 707.9519653320312]}]
digester. To use micro-aeration as an effective operational strategy in AD process, precise 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 13, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 31, "bbox_points": [318.0, 729.4679565429688, 330.0, 742.751953125]}]
 13


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 1, "bbox_points": [108.0, 72.34797668457031, 543.00048828125, 85.6319808959961]}]
dosing control is needed to prevent oxygen overdose and inhibition of obligate anaerobes. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 2, "bbox_points": [108.0, 92.98799133300781, 543.0, 106.2719955444336]}]
ORP or redox potential, which exhibits a logarithmic relationship with dissolved oxygen 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 3, "bbox_points": [108.0, 113.62800598144531, 542.9985961914062, 126.9120101928711]}]
(DO) concentration (See Appendix A), can be used for precise oxygen/air dosing control. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 4, "bbox_points": [108.0, 134.5080108642578, 542.998779296875, 147.79200744628906]}]
This approach was previously applied in biological hydrogen sulfide removal (Khanal 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [108.0, 155.1480255126953, 542.9974975585938, 168.43202209472656]}]
and Huang, 2003), biological nitrogen removal (Won and Ra, 2011), and pure culture 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [108.0, 175.78797912597656, 542.99951171875, 189.0719757080078]}]
fermentation processes to augment the product yields (Liu et al., 2013). However, 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [108.0, 196.42799377441406, 542.9967041015625, 209.7119903564453]}]
insights into the effects of ORP-controlled micro-aeration on aerobic-anaerobic metabolic 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [108.0, 217.30799865722656, 542.9968872070312, 230.5919952392578]}]
pathways and synergistic interactions between bacterial-archaeal populations in AD 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 9, "bbox_points": [108.0, 237.94801330566406, 211.95700073242188, 251.2320098876953]}]
process are lacking.   


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [108.0, 264.17999267578125, 255.0, 277.6319885253906]}]
1.2 Objectives of study 
 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 11, "bbox_points": [108.0, 297.22802734375, 539.9998779296875, 310.51202392578125]}]
The overall goal of this study was to develop and validate the performance of an ORP-


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [108.0, 318.1080017089844, 542.9992065429688, 331.3919982910156]}]
based micro-aeration system to control VFA accumulation and process stability without 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 13, "bbox_points": [108.0, 338.7480163574219, 542.9879150390625, 352.0320129394531]}]
the inhibition of methanogenesis during AD of lignocellulosic feedstock at high OLR. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 14, "bbox_points": [108.0, 359.38800048828125, 542.9959716796875, 372.6719970703125]}]
The ORP-based micro-aeration was also examined for AD process stability control. By 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 15, "bbox_points": [108.0, 380.02801513671875, 542.988525390625, 393.31201171875]}]
combining reactor performance and microbial community analyses with bioenergetics 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 16, "bbox_points": [108.0, 400.90802001953125, 539.9998779296875, 414.1920166015625]}]
and mass balance calculations, we provided new insights into the dynamics of ORP-


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 14, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 17, "bbox_points": [108.0, 421.5480041503906, 542.99462890625, 434.8320007324219]}]
controlled micro-aeration in AD process and presented an effective strategy for

2.3 Micro-aeration in AD processes 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 2, "bbox_points": [108.0, 102.82801818847656, 270.9609069824219, 116.11202239990234]}]
2.3.1 Micro-aerobic environment 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 3, "bbox_points": [108.0, 135.70802307128906, 542.99560546875, 148.9920196533203]}]
Micro-aeration is the dosing of small amount of air or oxygen into an anaerobic system. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 4, "bbox_points": [108.0, 156.3479766845703, 542.9954223632812, 169.63197326660156]}]
Different homologous terminologies are being used, namely micro-aeration, limited 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [108.0, 177.2279815673828, 543.0, 190.51197814941406]}]
aeration, micro-oxygenation, oxygenation, and microaerobic condition among others 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [108.0, 197.8679962158203, 542.9925537109375, 211.15199279785156]}]
(Krayzelova et al., 2015). Air or oxygen could be dosed either one time, intermittently 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [108.0, 218.5080108642578, 542.9929809570312, 231.79200744628906]}]
(pulse-mode) or continuously at different stages of the AD process (pretreatment, during 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [108.0, 239.1480255126953, 542.9974975585938, 252.43202209472656]}]

vaguely defined due to lack of standard method to precisely monitor oxygen level in the 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [108.0, 280.6679992675781, 542.9994506835938, 293.9519958496094]}]
AD system. In aerobic processes such as aerobic fermentation or activated sludge

process, DO – residual oxygen concentration in aqueous phase – is used as a parameter to 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 24, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [108.0, 321.9480285644531, 542.985595703125, 335.2320251464844]}]

probe lacks sensitivity needed to precisely quantify microaerobic environment in an AD

2.3.5 Micro-aeration process control 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 2, "bbox_points": [108.0, 103.06800842285156, 543.0006103515625, 116.35201263427734]}]
Process control is critically important to allow precise control of micro-aeration in AD 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 3, "bbox_points": [108.0, 123.70802307128906, 542.996826171875, 136.9920196533203]}]
system. Besides time-based on-off control, other fine process control systems such as 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 4, "bbox_points": [108.0, 144.3479766845703, 542.997314453125, 157.63197326660156]}]

[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [108.0, 165.2279815673828, 542.9951171875, 178.51197814941406]}]
(Chang et al., 2014; Khanal and Huang, 2006; Nghiem et al., 2014) are recommended. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [108.0, 185.8679962158203, 542.98681640625, 199.15199279785156]}]
The use of ORP as a control parameter could maintain enough residual oxygen without 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [108.0, 206.5080108642578, 542.9874877929688, 219.79200744628906]}]
inhibiting obligate anaerobes, since dosing of oxygen could be controlled precisely based 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [108.0, 227.1480255126953, 542.9856567382812, 240.43202209472656]}]
on oxygen consumption rate of facultative bacteria in the system. Application of ORP for 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 9, "bbox_points": [108.0, 248.02796936035156, 542.9927978515625, 261.3119812011719]}]
process monitoring and control of fermentation process for producing desired products by 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [108.0, 268.6679992675781, 542.9892578125, 281.9519958496094]}]
sensing intracellular metabolic profile was also implemented in microaerobic and 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 11, "bbox_points": [108.0, 289.3080139160156, 542.998291015625, 302.5920104980469]}]
anaerobic environments (Liu et al., 2013). For these reasons, ORP-based process control 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [108.0, 309.9480285644531, 543.0, 323.2320251464844]}]
system appears to be the most appropriate technique for controlling micro-aeration. 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 13, "bbox_points": [108.0, 330.8280029296875, 542.9951171875, 344.11199951171875]}]
However, ORP is also susceptible to environmental factors such as pH, temperature, and 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 14, "bbox_points": [108.0, 351.468017578125, 539.9998779296875, 364.75201416015625]}]
ionic strength. Therefore, further development and application of ORP-based micro-


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 15, "bbox_points": [108.0, 371.1600036621094, 408.8939208984375, 385.2239990234375]}]
aeration control strategy should be examined for AD system.   


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 16, "bbox_points": [108.0, 402.8280029296875, 542.98681640625, 416.11199951171875]}]
Selection between air and pure oxygen for dosing into the anaerobic reactor should be 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 17, "bbox_points": [108.0, 423.468017578125, 542.9925537109375, 436.75201416015625]}]
based on the purpose of the micro-aeration and the associated cost. Use of air is much 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 18, "bbox_points": [108.0, 444.1080017089844, 542.9879150390625, 457.3919982910156]}]
cheaper than using pure oxygen. However, air dilutes the biogas with nitrogen, which 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 19, "bbox_points": [108.0, 464.9880065917969, 539.9998779296875, 478.2720031738281]}]
ultimately deters the quality of biogas as an energy resource. If the sole purpose of micro-


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 20, "bbox_points": [108.0, 485.6280212402344, 542.9902954101562, 498.9120178222656]}]
aeration is to increase the VFA production instead of biogas production, air could be used 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 21, "bbox_points": [108.0, 506.2679748535156, 542.9968872070312, 519.5519409179688]}]
for micro-aeration. Díaz et al. (2015) suggested the application of oxygen at 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 22, "bbox_points": [108.0, 526.907958984375, 542.9866943359375, 540.1919555664062]}]
concentration of 95% (by volume), which was generated from pressure swing adsorption 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 23, "bbox_points": [108.0, 546.8399658203125, 426.5248107910156, 560.9039916992188]}]
generator as a more economical substitute for air or pure oxygen.  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 24, "bbox_points": [108.0, 578.5079956054688, 542.998291015625, 591.7919921875]}]
The challenge associated with automatic process control of micro-aeration is the bottle 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 25, "bbox_points": [108.0, 599.14794921875, 542.9953002929688, 612.4319458007812]}]
neck of the implementation of micro-aeration at full-scale AD system. The development 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 43, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 26, "bbox_points": [108.0, 619.7879638671875, 542.9986572265625, 633.0719604492188]}]

stability is required to make this technology successful.

 43


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 1, "bbox_points": [108.0, 72.10798645019531, 540.6561889648438, 85.3919906616211]}]
2.4 Syntrophic relationships of microbial community under micro-aerobic condition 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 2, "bbox_points": [108.0, 103.06800842285156, 542.9879150390625, 116.35201263427734]}]
The foundation of AD process is the breakdown of complex organic matters in series of 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 3, "bbox_points": [108.0, 123.70802307128906, 543.0050048828125, 136.9920196533203]}]
steps, in which products of the previous step are substrates for the following step. As a 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 4, "bbox_points": [108.0, 144.3479766845703, 542.9942626953125, 157.63197326660156]}]
result, microorganisms interact with each other in such a way that allows the continuous 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 5, "bbox_points": [108.0, 165.2279815673828, 542.9879150390625, 178.51197814941406]}]
substrate flow from hydrolysis to fermentation and then methane generation. This 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 6, "bbox_points": [108.0, 185.8679962158203, 542.9807739257812, 199.15199279785156]}]
syntrophic relationship between bacteria and archaea community allows them to survive 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 7, "bbox_points": [108.0, 206.5080108642578, 542.9993896484375, 219.79200744628906]}]
even with limited ATP production from thermodynamic marginal reactions (Stams and 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 8, "bbox_points": [108.0, 226.90797424316406, 184.33009338378906, 240.1919708251953]}]
Plugge, 2009).  


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 9, "bbox_points": [108.0, 257.8680114746094, 539.9998779296875, 271.1520080566406]}]
The AD process is a slow-rate process (with hydrolysis or methanogenesis as rate-


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 10, "bbox_points": [108.0, 278.7480163574219, 542.99560546875, 292.0320129394531]}]
limiting steps, depending on substrate type) and is highly susceptible to changes in 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 11, "bbox_points": [108.0, 299.38800048828125, 542.9923095703125, 312.6719970703125]}]
environmental conditions including inhibitory compounds produced during AD such as 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 12, "bbox_points": [108.0, 320.02801513671875, 542.99609375, 333.8706359863281]}]
H2S, NH3 and VFA (Chen et al., 2008). Micro-aeration overcomes these impediments by 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 13, "bbox_points": [108.0, 340.6679992675781, 542.9854125976562, 353.9519958496094]}]
augmenting the abundance and activity of the targeted group of microorganisms in order 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 14, "bbox_points": [108.0, 361.3080139160156, 542.9796142578125, 374.5920104980469]}]
to achieve the specific objective. For example, it stimulates the production of 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 15, "bbox_points": [108.0, 382.1880187988281, 542.9854736328125, 395.4720153808594]}]
extracellular hydrolytic enzymes in hydrolytic bacteria thereby enhancing substrate 


[{"type": "pdf_block", "pdf_page": 44, "pdf_page_label": "", "block": 16, "bbox_points": [108.0, 402.8280029296875, 542.9953002929688, 416.11199951171875]}]
solubilization and VFA production or promote the activity of sulfide-oxidizing bacteria
